# V1 to V2 generation comparison: https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1692427/000119312526252096/d23867dex21.htm

V1: run eaafcac8-790b-41bb-a5e1-b12187a55e7d (LEGAL_SCHEMA/V1). V2: run 39456f2d-4c92-46c9-8398-2d55c5b0a58f (LEGAL_SCHEMA/V2). Both generations share one agreement structure, so sections correspond node for node.

## Summary

| Measure | V1 | V2 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Sections routed to a family | 95 of 104 | 93 of 104 |
| Proposed facts | 1039 | 995 |
| Valid facts | 982 | 850 |
| Held by validation | 57 | 145 |
| Facts with a component tree | 0 | 850 |

| V1 valid facts | Count |
| --- | --- |
| With a V2 counterpart (half or more of the cited bytes) | 649 |
| Partial counterpart (some cited bytes) | 231 |
| No V2 counterpart | 102 |
| ... of which overlap a fact V2 held | 84 |

V2 valid facts with no V1 counterpart (new to V2): 32 of 850. Sections whose routing is the same in both: 61; differs: 43. The V1 review carries 38 decided or commented items; each is shown beside its V1 fact below.

## Why the two results differ

- The schemas differ by design: a V1 fact is one sentence with roles; a V2 fact is a headline over a tree of verbatim components with byte spans, laid out as coded tables. Counterparts are found on the shared bytes, not on wording.
- A section routed to more families in V2 (the routing widening by code and the V2 schema's subtypes) yields facts V1 never asked for; a section routed to fewer yields facts V1 had and V2 does not.
- A V2 fact held by validation (an unresolved component span, an invented role, an invalid tree) is not shown; a V1 fact over the same bytes then has no visible counterpart. The held fact and its code are listed under the section.
- The V2 tables place only facts carrying a readout; a V2 fact without one is listed under its section as evidence without a readout, with its V1 counterparts.
